% =============================================================================
\section{Analysis Pipeline}
\label{sec:pipeline}
% =============================================================================

The primary measurement is produced by a single driver, \texttt{run\_analysis.sh},
which chains the selection, the per-observable cross-section extraction, and the
comparison/diagnostic stages:

\begin{verbatim}
selection/ccpi_selection.C   ->  histograms.root + unfolding_inputs.root
                                  | (5 observables x 8 methods)
xsec/ccpi_xsec.C(obs,method) ->  xsec_{obs}_{method}.root  (40 files)
xsec/compare_unfolding.C     ->  compare_plots/
xsec/compare_generators.C    ->  gen_compare/
selection/event_display.C    ->  event_displays/
\end{verbatim}

Each output file is validated for freshness and non-zero content after the step
that produces it.  The official-framework cross-check of
Section~\ref{sec:nuwroclosure} is driven separately
(\texttt{xsec\_analyzer/run\_nuwro\_observables.sh}).

% =============================================================================
\section{Binning Optimisation, Systematic Breakdown, and Threshold Validation}
\label{sec:binopt}
% =============================================================================

The reconstructed spectra, response matrices, efficiencies, and differential
cross sections shown throughout this note are the \textsc{XSecAnalyzer} framework
outputs on the \emph{optimised} binning defined here; the four-generator
comparisons of Fig.~\ref{fig:nuwro_closure} onward likewise use it.

\subsection{Momentum-threshold validation}
The two signal-definition momentum thresholds sit at the selection efficiency
turn-on, measured against a threshold-free true signal (the CC $\nu_\mu$
$1\mu1\pi^\pm$ topology with the momentum/angle cuts removed):

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
Threshold & below & above & turn-on \\
\midrule
$p_\mu > 0.15$~GeV/c  & $\sim$0--1.5\% & $\sim$9--12\%  & 0.145$\to$0.155 \\
$p_\pi > 0.175$~GeV/c & $\sim$0--3\%   & $\sim$12--16\% & 0.165$\to$0.185 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Both are correctly placed, lowering them would add near-zero-efficiency
phase space, raising them would discard measurable signal. The reconstructed pion
momentum (muon-hypothesis range) is biased low and increasingly so with momentum
(mean reco$-$true $\approx0$ at threshold, $-0.15$~GeV/c at 0.40) from the
$\mu$-vs-$\pi$ mass hypothesis plus pion re-interaction; a pion-hypothesis mass
correction is applied to the $p_\pi$ reco bins.

\subsection{Binning optimisation}
Bins were designed to be at least the local RMS detector resolution wide (so the
response stays well conditioned and the Wiener-SVD does not amplify the correlated
flux/cross-section uncertainty) and to hold $\geq45$ selected-signal events
(statistical floor). This lowers the total fractional uncertainty in every
observable, chiefly through the statistical and unfolding-amplified terms; the
flux/beamline term is correlated across bins and sets a floor:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
Observable & bins & Total before & Total after \\
\midrule
$p_\mu$          & 22$\to$7 & 33.2\% & \textbf{25.0\%} \\
$p_\pi$          & 5$\to$5  & 27.7\% & 27.0\% \\
$\cos\theta_\mu$ & 12$\to$5 & 31.3\% & \textbf{26.7\%} \\
$\cos\theta_\pi$ & 12$\to$5 & 48.5\% & \textbf{33.9\%} \\
$\theta_{\mu\pi}$& 9$\to$7  & 28.4\% & \textbf{23.5\%} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection{Systematic breakdown by source}
The framework builds a covariance matrix for every systematic; the source-group
decomposition method is illustrated below on a single configuration (the
definitive per-configuration breakdowns for the full FHC, RHC, and combined
exposures are Tables~\ref{tab:systbreak_fhc}--\ref{tab:systbreak_comb}). The
bin-averaged fractional uncertainty from each source group (on the optimised
binning) is:

\begin{center}\small
\captionof{table}{Bin-averaged fractional systematic uncertainty (\%) by source
  group on the optimised binning.}
\label{tab:systbreak}
\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}
\toprule
Source & $p_\mu$ & $p_\pi$ & $\cos\theta_\mu$ & $\cos\theta_\pi$ & $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ \\
\midrule
\textbf{Total}         & 27.2 & 28.3 & 28.8 & 33.8 & 25.1 \\
Cross section (GENIE)  & 8.6  & 8.5  & 9.1  & 9.5  & 8.1 \\
Flux, PPFX          & 21.8 & 23.6 & 20.7 & 25.4 & 18.7 \\
Flux, beamline geom.& 2.3  & 2.2  & 2.3  & 3.3  & 1.6 \\
Detector               & 9.6  & 10.2 & 14.0 & 14.6 & 10.5 \\
Reinteraction          & 3.2  & 2.6  & 3.4  & 3.4  & 2.5 \\
MC + data stats        & 8.6  & 6.5  & 9.4  & 11.9 & 9.3 \\
POT + targets          & 2.7  & 2.9  & 2.7  & 3.0  & 2.4 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The NuMI flux uncertainty has two parts, listed separately above: the
PPFX hadron-production term (the dominant systematic, 19--26\%) and the
beamline-geometry term. The latter is built from the 12 geometry-variation weights
(\texttt{Horn\_2kA}, \texttt{Horn1/2\_x/y\_3mm}, \texttt{Beam\_spot\_*},
\texttt{Beam\_shift\_*}, \texttt{Horns\_*\_water}, \texttt{Target\_z\_7mm})
injected into the numuMC overlay via \texttt{AddBeamlineGeometryWeights}, summed in
quadrature as two-sided unisim variations. It is sub-dominant, $1.6$--$3.3\%$
across the observables. (Building it required a framework fix: the covariance
code dereferenced a null histogram when a systematic, here the beamline weights,
present in the numuMC but not the dirt overlay, was absent from some MC sample;
it now falls back to that sample's central value, i.e.\ unit weight.) The per-bin
breakdowns are shown in Fig.~\ref{fig:systbreak}.

\begin{figure}[H]\centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.32\textwidth]{fw_systbreak_fhc}\hfill
  \includegraphics[width=0.32\textwidth]{fw_systbreak_rhc}\hfill
  \includegraphics[width=0.32\textwidth]{fw_systbreak_comb}
  \caption{Bin-averaged fractional systematic uncertainty by source vs.\ observable,
    for FHC (left), RHC (centre) and combined (right); the figure form of
    Tables~\ref{tab:systbreak_fhc}--\ref{tab:systbreak_comb}. Colours use the
    Okabe-Ito colourblind-safe palette with distinct markers per source: total
    (black), flux (blue), detector (green), cross section (vermillion),
    reinteraction (purple), data stat (orange).}
  \label{fig:systbreak}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Anatomy of the flux term: genuine flux vs.\ unfolding amplification}
\label{sec:fluxanatomy}

The flux (PPFX) term dominates the budget, and at $17$--$26\%$ it is larger than
the $\sim\!17\%$ normalisation uncertainty usually quoted for the NuMI flux. It is
therefore worth asking how much of it is the flux model itself and how much is
statistical amplification introduced by the unfolding. To separate the two we
evaluate the flux covariance at two stages, on the reconstructed spectrum
(before unfolding) and on the unfolded result, and, at each stage, both
integrated (the fully correlated, normalisation-like number, from the sum of all
covariance elements) and bin-averaged (the mean of the diagonal fractional errors,
i.e.\ the number quoted in Table~\ref{tab:systbreak} and Fig.~\ref{fig:systbreak}).

\begin{center}
\captionof{table}{Flux (PPFX) fractional uncertainty before and after unfolding.
  The reconstructed-level value is $\sim\!17\%$ and flat across observables; the
  genuine flux-normalisation uncertainty. The unfolded value is that same $\sim\!17\%$
  amplified by the Wiener--SVD propagation through the finite-statistics response;
  the amplification tracks the data$+$MC statistical quality of each observable.}
\label{tab:fluxanatomy}
\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}
\toprule
 & $p_\mu$ & $p_\pi$ & $\cos\theta_\mu$ & $\cos\theta_\pi$ & $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ \\
\midrule
Flux, reco (integrated / norm) & 16.9 & 17.0 & 16.7 & 16.7 & 16.7 \\
Flux, reco (bin-averaged)      & 17.7 & 16.4 & 16.6 & 17.0 & 17.0 \\
\midrule
Flux, unfolded (bin-averaged)  & 19.8 & 22.4 & 19.8 & 25.5 & 17.5 \\
Amplification factor           & 1.12 & 1.37 & 1.20 & 1.49 & 1.03 \\
Excess from unfolding (abs.)   & +2.1 & +6.0 & +3.2 & +8.5 & +0.5 \\
\midrule
Data$+$MC stats (unfolded)     & 7.9  & 6.3  & 8.4  & 12.2 & 8.8 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Three points follow. First, the \emph{genuine} flux uncertainty is
$\sim\!17\%$ and identical for all five observables, as expected for a
normalisation term. Second, it is not itself contaminated by Monte Carlo
statistics: at reco level the integrated (correlated) and bin-averaged (diagonal)
numbers agree to better than a percent, whereas statistical noise leaking into the
covariance would inflate the diagonal well above the correlated norm. This is
expected; the $\sim\!600$ PPFX universes reweight the \emph{same} events, so
there is no independent per-universe statistical scatter. Third, the elevated
unfolded values are the $\sim\!17\%$ flux \emph{amplified} by the Wiener--SVD
propagation $A_C M\,V_\text{flux}\,M^\top A_C^\top$ through the finite-statistics
response. It is at this step, not in the flux multisim, that the response
statistics transfer into the flux term: the amplification is largest exactly where
the statistics are thinnest ($\cos\theta_\pi$: stat.\ $12.2\%$, amplification
$1.49\times$, $+8.5\%$) and negligible where the response is well conditioned
($\theta_{\mu\pi}$: $1.03\times$, $+0.5\%$). The lever for reducing the flux term is
therefore the response conditioning (more overlay statistics, or the observable's
binning and regularisation strength), not the flux model; $\cos\theta_\pi$; the
noisiest observable across the entire budget, is where it would matter most.

\subsection{Multi-run overlay and a resonance-model closure}
\label{sec:multirun}

Two facts frame what more statistics can and cannot do for the flux term. First,
the flux \emph{systematic} is a normalisation-and-shape model uncertainty and is
therefore POT-independent: adding data or overlay does not change it. Second,
Section~\ref{sec:fluxanatomy} showed that the elevated \emph{unfolded} flux is that
$\sim\!17\%$ model uncertainty amplified by the Wiener--SVD propagation through a
statistically-limited response, and that the limitation is the \emph{data}
statistics, not the overlay. Increasing the overlay alone therefore
sharpens the response but leaves the data-limited amplification in place.

To study a genuine multi-run configuration we combine the FHC overlays for
Runs~1, 2 and~4 (with \texttt{Run4}$=$\texttt{Run4c}$+$\texttt{Run4d} counted once;
the file first distributed as ``Run~5 FHC'' was set aside on inspection, being in
fact an intrinsic-$\nu_e$ overlay, $86\%$ $\nu_e$, $14\%$ $\bar\nu_e$, no
$\nu_\mu$ events, hence the large $\nu_e$-enriched POT-equivalent and no $\nu_\mu$
signal; the genuine Run~5 $\nu_\mu$ overlay, a \texttt{reweightedPPFX} sample, was
subsequently located and is used for the extended set below),
$2.32\times10^{6}$ unique events, normalised to the FHC data POT
$6.626\times10^{20}$ (Section~\ref{sec:nueconsistency}). Because CC$\pi^{\pm}$ is
resonance-dominated, robustness to the resonant axial mass is tested with
Poisson-thrown fake data reweighted to $M_A^\mathrm{RES}=1.12\pm0.22$~GeV
($\pm1\sigma$; axial dipole ratio in the true interaction $Q^2$, a
$+8.1\%/-7.2\%$ rate distortion). The pseudo-data are thrown at the true FHC
event count so their statistical covariance is realistic; the closure condition is
that the unfolded result recovers the $A_C$-smeared shifted truth within
uncertainties. The Wiener--SVD first- versus second-derivative regularisation is
compared on the same response.

The closure succeeds for every observable, both signs of the shift, and both
regularisations: the unfolded result recovers the $A_C$-smeared shifted truth with
$\chi^2/\mathrm{ndf}<1$ throughout (Table~\ref{tab:mares_closure}), all $p$-values
above $0.63$ and most above $0.9$. The Wiener--SVD machinery is therefore unbiased
under a $\pm1\sigma$ $M_A^\mathrm{RES}$ distortion, and the first- and
second-derivative regularisations perform equivalently in the closure (the
first-derivative variant carries a slightly larger statistical term). Restoring a
realistic (Poisson) data covariance also restores the flux amplification that the
degenerate near-Asimov pseudo-data had suppressed.

To separate the mean amplification from single-realisation noise, we throw $15$
independent Poisson pseudo-datasets (central value only, no $M_A^\mathrm{RES}$
shift, at the same $6.626\times10^{20}$ FHC exposure) and unfold each
(Table~\ref{tab:amp_throws}). Averaged over throws, the unfolded flux stabilises at
$24.7$--$27.1\%$, a mean amplification of $1.44$--$1.63\times$ over the
$\sim\!17\%$ floor, with a data-statistical term of $8$--$13\%$. This confirms that
the amplification is driven by the data statistics, not the overlay, and is
consistent with the mechanism of Section~\ref{sec:fluxanatomy}. The
throw-to-throw scatter is small for the momenta ($p_\mu$, $p_\pi$: $\pm0.06$--$0.08\times$,
flux $\pm1.1$--$1.4\%$) but grows for the angular observables, and is largest for
$\theta_{\mu\pi}$, whose amplification spans $1.27$--$2.02\times$ (flux
$21.4$--$34.2\%$) across the $15$ throws. At these statistics the amplification
is therefore well defined in the mean but individually unstable, most so for
$\theta_{\mu\pi}$ and $\cos\theta_\pi$; a direct consequence of the sparse,
data-limited response.

\begin{center}\small
\captionof{table}{Flux amplification averaged over $15$ independent Poisson-thrown
  pseudo-datasets at the FHC data POT: mean $\pm$ standard deviation and
  min--max range over throws, for the unfolded flux (bin-averaged) and the
  amplification relative to the $\sim\!17\%$ reco-level floor.}
\label{tab:amp_throws}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
Observable & Amplification (mean$\pm$sd) & Amp.\ range & Unfolded flux & Flux range \\
\midrule
$p_\mu$          & $1.44\pm0.08\times$ & $[1.27,1.60]$ & $24.7\pm1.4\%$ & $[21.9,27.5]$ \\
$p_\pi$          & $1.52\pm0.06\times$ & $[1.40,1.65]$ & $26.2\pm1.1\%$ & $[24.0,28.4]$ \\
$\cos\theta_\mu$ & $1.60\pm0.14\times$ & $[1.41,1.85]$ & $26.8\pm2.3\%$ & $[23.7,31.1]$ \\
$\cos\theta_\pi$ & $1.63\pm0.11\times$ & $[1.45,1.89]$ & $27.1\pm1.9\%$ & $[24.1,31.6]$ \\
$\theta_{\mu\pi}$& $1.58\pm0.21\times$ & $[1.27,2.02]$ & $26.7\pm3.6\%$ & $[21.4,34.2]$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{center}\small
\captionof{table}{Fake-data closure: $\chi^2/\mathrm{ndf}$ of the unfolded result
  against the $A_C$-smeared $M_A^\mathrm{RES}$-shifted truth, for the two shift
  signs and the two Wiener--SVD regularisations (number of bins in parentheses).
  All values are below unity per degree of freedom ($p>0.63$).}
\label{tab:mares_closure}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
Observable & $+1\sigma$ (2nd) & $+1\sigma$ (1st) & $-1\sigma$ (2nd) & $-1\sigma$ (1st) \\
\midrule
$p_\mu$ (7)          & 1.26 & 2.15 & 0.95 & 2.14 \\
$p_\pi$ (5)          & 1.24 & 3.44 & 0.25 & 0.39 \\
$\cos\theta_\mu$ (5) & 0.47 & 0.63 & 1.90 & 1.24 \\
$\cos\theta_\pi$ (5) & 2.99 & 1.07 & 0.24 & 0.33 \\
$\theta_{\mu\pi}$ (7)& 1.71 & 3.32 & 1.96 & 4.33 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Adding the genuine Run~5 $\nu_\mu$ overlay extends the set to
$\{$Run~1, 2, 4, 5$\}$, $2.87\times10^{6}$ events ($+24\%$), combined MC POT
$8.549\times10^{21}$, at the full FHC data POT
$8.857\times10^{20}$. The extraction closes for every observable
($\chi^2/\mathrm{ndf}<1$, $p>0.92$) and the flux term ($22$--$29\%$ unfolded,
amplification $1.29$--$1.72\times$) is statistically consistent with the
$\{$Run~1, 2, 4$\}$ throws above: the extra $24\%$ of overlay does not move the
data-limited amplification, as expected, but the measurement now spans the
complete FHC exposure.

\subsection{Consistency with the sibling $\nu_e\,\mathrm{CC}\,1\pi$ measurement}
\label{sec:nueconsistency}

This analysis is one of a pair of MicroBooNE NuMI CC single-charged-pion
measurements; the companion $\nu_e\,\mathrm{CC}\,1\pi$ analysis shares the same
cross-section-extraction framework, beam and reconstruction. A cross-check against
its internal note confirms consistency on the essential conventions and identifies
the differences that a combined NuMI CC$\pi$ programme should reconcile.

\emph{Consistent by construction.} Both use the Wiener--SVD extraction framework;
the updated NuMI flux with the fixed geometry bug and Geant~v4.10.4; the same
signal topology (exactly one $\pi^\pm$, zero $\pi^0$ or heavier mesons, any number
of protons, charged current), and the same systematic suite, PPFX flux
hadronic universes plus the \emph{same 12 beamline-geometry unisims}, the
600-universe GENIE cross-section multisim with unisims, the Geant4 reinteraction
reweighting, the standard detector-variation set, and $2\%$ / $1\%$ / $100\%$
POT / target / dirt normalisation uncertainties. The FHC beam-on data POT per run
agrees exactly (Run~2 $1.268$, Run~4 $2.075\times10^{20}$).

\emph{Differences reconciled or flagged.} (i)~\textbf{POT accounting:} the
good-POT is the same beam data for the $\nu_e$ and $\nu_\mu$ analyses, so the
exposures are identical; Run~1 FHC $=3.283\times10^{20}$ (standard trigger; the
open-trigger era is treated separately), giving a total FHC exposure of
$8.857\times10^{20}$ over Runs~1--5. (ii)~\textbf{Run~4:} the \texttt{Run4} overlay
is identical to \texttt{Run4c}$+$\texttt{Run4d} combined (same $859{,}368$ events
and $2.834\times10^{21}$ POT); only one may be used to avoid double counting.
(iii)~\textbf{Pion threshold:} $\nu_e$ uses $\mathrm{KE}_\pi>40$~MeV
($p_\pi>0.113\GeVc$) versus our $p_\pi>0.175\GeVc$
(Table~\ref{tab:pithresh}). (iv)~\textbf{Opening-angle cut:} $\nu_e$ excludes
$\theta_{e\pi}>170^\circ$ (a shower-specific back-to-back misreconstruction),
whereas our muon-track topology uses $\theta_{\mu\pi}<2.6$~rad ($149^\circ$).
(v)~\textbf{Regularisation:} the $\nu_e$ analysis smooths on the first derivative,
this analysis on the second (Section~\ref{sec:multirun}). (vi)~\textbf{Scope:}
both analyses cover Runs~1--5 in FHC and RHC; the present measurement reports FHC,
RHC, and their combination.

% =============================================================================
\section{Limitations and Open Questions}
\label{sec:limitations}
% =============================================================================

Four things are not settled. They are collected here rather than left in the
sections that produced them, because each is large enough to bear on how the
numbers in this note should be read.

\paragraph{A uniform 13\% Wiener--SVD normalisation offset.}
On fake data the truth is known exactly, so the unfolded integral should reproduce
it. It does not: the ratio is $1.13$ in FHC, RHC \emph{and} combined. The offset is
the same order as the physics differences this analysis sets out to measure --- the
RHC/FHC truth ratio itself is $0.785$ --- so it cannot be dismissed as a small
correction.

Two features make it more interesting than a simple scale error. It is
\emph{uniform}: before the combined-configuration detector systematic was corrected
(\S\ref{sec:combtruth}) the three ratios were $1.14$, $1.03$ and $1.08$, and that
correction brought them into agreement without being aimed at them. And it is not
the additional smearing, since $A_C$ is applied to every prediction curve and
cancels in a comparison of unfolded data against $A_C$-smeared truth.

What it is not: not a POT or flux error (the exposures sum exactly and each
configuration carries its own flux), not a detector-variation artefact (it survives
with the detVar term removed entirely), and not a response-matrix pathology (the
column-normalised diagonals agree across configurations to three decimal places).
It is stated here as unexplained.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{An inconsistency in the $A_C$ normalisation.}{An inconsistency in the A-C normalisation.}}
For the proton-tagged $p_\mu$ in FHC the fake-data truth integrates to $0.476$, the
unfolded result to $0.553$, and $A_C$ applied to that truth to $0.376$. If the
Wiener--SVD estimate satisfies $\hat{x}=A_C\,x_\mathrm{true}$, the unfolded result
should sit at $0.79\times$ the truth; it sits at $1.16\times$. Both cannot hold.

One caveat limits how far this can be pushed with the present outputs: the $A_C$
written to the closure sidecar is restricted to the slice, while the smearing is
applied in the full true-bin space before slicing, so the operation cannot be
reproduced exactly from that file. A related hypothesis --- that the fake-data truth
is missing the $A_C$ smearing applied to every other prediction --- was tested
directly and refuted: applying it moves the closure ratio from $1.09$--$1.24$ to
$1.54$--$1.60$, further from unity. Resolving this needs the convention settled at
the source rather than inferred downstream.

\paragraph{One detector systematic still sits outside its inputs.}
After the per-mode detVar pooling fix, five of the six inclusive observables have a
combined detector uncertainty bracketed by their FHC and RHC values, as a combined
exposure should. $\cos\theta_\mu$ does not: $23.7\%$ against inputs of $26.1\%$ and
$32.5\%$. This is small next to the $41.4\%$ it carried before the fix, but the
direction is unexplained.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\theta_\mu$ carries no shape information in FHC.}{theta-mu carries no shape information in FHC.}}
The additional smearing matrix for FHC $\theta_\mu$ is rank-1 to numerical
precision: its second singular value is $2.8\times10^{-6}$ of the first, against
$\sim\!5\times10^{-2}$ for the inputs. Every model and the data are mapped onto one
curve, so the apparent agreement between them in that observable is by construction
rather than physical. Any statement that $\theta_\mu$ repairs what $A_C$ does to
$\cos\theta_\mu$ is withdrawn; the two are not independent tests.

\section{Summary}
\label{sec:summary}
% =============================================================================

We have presented a simultaneous measurement of five CC$\pi^{\pm}$ differential cross
sections on argon using the full MicroBooNE NuMI exposure in three configurations
FHC ($8.857\times10^{20}$~POT), RHC ($1.108\times10^{21}$~POT), and their
combination ($1.994\times10^{21}$~POT); the first such multi-observable
measurement in the MicroBooNE NuMI dataset.

\paragraph{Selection.}
The event selection achieves a signal purity of $\approx\!55\%$ and a flat
efficiency of $\sim\!13$--$20\%$ in the measured phase space (reference phase space:
$57.2\%$ purity, $12.1$--$12.6\%$ efficiency; Table~\ref{tab:phasespace}), identical
across the three configurations since the selection is the same. The blind
data/prediction ratio of $\approx\!0.75$ is consistent with the known GENIE~v3
over-prediction of CC$\pi^{\pm}$ production documented in
MiniBooNE~\cite{miniboone_ccpi}, MINERvA~\cite{minerva_ccpi},
T2K~\cite{t2k_ccpip}, and the MicroBooNE BNB CC$\pi^+$ measurement~\cite{uboone_bnb2026_ccpi}.

\paragraph{Cross-section results, framework validation, and phase space.}
Differential cross sections are extracted via a framework-consistent Wiener-SVD
unfolder using each configuration's own integrated $\nu_\mu + \bar{\nu}_\mu$ flux
(FHC $\Phi = 6.81159\times10^{-10}$~cm$^{-2}$~POT$^{-1}$, $\bar{\nu}_\mu$ fraction
$34.9\%$; RHC and combined in Sec.~\ref{sec:flux}).  At a common \emph{reference} phase space ($p_\mu,p_\pi>0.1\GeVc$, reco
$\thmupM<2.6$~rad), the custom pipeline was validated against the fully
independent official XSecAnalyzer framework to $\le2\%$ on every observable
($d\sigma/d\pmuM$: 1.016, $d\sigma/d\ppiM$: 0.979, $d\sigma/d\cthmuM$: 0.987,
$d\sigma/d\cthpiM$: 1.004, $d\sigma/d\thmupM$: 1.001) after three corrections
(Section~\ref{sec:datamc}): importing the full systematic covariance into the
Wiener filter (a stat-only covariance over-corrects by $\sim26\%$), cancelling the
EXT subtraction for the pure-MC fake data ($\sim3.7\%$), and matched binning; the
two pipelines were also shown to select identical events (1914 vs 1913.6 NuWro
events at the final cut).  The final results (Table~\ref{tab:sigint_all}) then refine
the \emph{measured phase space} to $p_\mu>0.15$, $p_\pi>0.175\GeVc$,
$\theta_{\mu\pi}<2.6$~rad, chosen from the efficiency turn-on curves so the
selection efficiency is flat at $\sim$13--20\% (Section~\ref{sec:signal}): the
momentum thresholds remove the $\sim$5\% low-momentum bins and the $2.6$~rad cut
rejects the cosmic-dominated large-angle region.  No lower opening-angle cut is
applied, since the small-angle region is high-purity.  This raises the efficiency
from $12.1\%$ to $15.6\%$ at $\sim$55\% purity.  The integrated values are
correspondingly smaller and, being a different phase space, are not directly
comparable to the framework's full-$0.1\GeVc$ numbers.

\paragraph{Context in the experimental landscape.}
The measured $\sigma_{\mathrm{int}}$ corresponds to $\approx 0.65$--$0.70$ times
the GENIE~v3 prediction, consistent with generator over-predictions of
20--35\% seen by all prior experiments.  This
measurement provides:
\begin{itemize}[noitemsep]
  \item The first CC$\pi^{\pm}$ differential cross sections from the MicroBooNE
    NuMI (off-axis) dataset, complementing the existing BNB-beam CC$\pi^+$
    result~\cite{uboone_bnb2026_ccpi} from the same detector.
  \item Constraints at lower neutrino energies ($E_\nu \sim 0.1$--$0.5$~GeV)
    than the previous NuMI CC$\pi^+$ results (ArgoNEUT~\cite{argoneut_ccpi} at
    $\langle E_\nu \rangle \approx 9.6$~GeV, MINERvA~\cite{minerva_ccpi} at
    $\langle E_\nu \rangle \approx 3.6$~GeV).
  \item A measurement on an argon target at the kinematic regime most relevant
    to the planned DUNE~\cite{dune} oscillation analysis, which operates at a
    similar beam energy with a LArTPC far detector.
  \item Sensitivity to the $\bar{\nu}_\mu$ contribution ($\approx 38\%$) in
    CC$\pi^{\pm}$ production on argon, previously unmeasured.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Required for publication.}
Native systematic propagation in the custom pipeline (flux, cross-section, and
detector-response multisims, currently imported from the framework for the
unfolding covariance) and inclusion of Runs~2, 4, and~5 beam-on data
($\approx 5.4\times$ additional exposure) are needed before a real-data result can
be published.  With the EXT cancellation and full covariance in place the unfolded
spectra are everywhere positive and stable; the remaining $\le2\%$ residual against
the framework is dominated by the covariance-import approximation
(Section~\ref{sec:datamc}).  A comparison with NEUT and GiBUU predictions is also
planned.

% =============================================================================
\appendix

\section{Identity closure: unfolding the prediction with itself}
\label{sec:identity}

The fake-data closure tests reported in the body compare an unfolded Poisson throw against
the truth it was thrown from. They therefore test the whole chain at once, and a modest
$\chi^2$ can hide a genuine inconsistency inside the unfolding itself, because the
statistical fluctuation of the throw is the dominant term.

The test here removes that fluctuation. Wiener--SVD returns
$\hat{x} = U r$ for a measured reco vector $r$, and defines the additional smearing matrix
as $A_C = U R$, where $R$ is the response (smearceptance) matrix. Feeding an \emph{exact}
prediction $r = R\,t$ back through the chain must therefore return
\begin{equation}
  \hat{x} = U R\, t = A_C\, t
\end{equation}
identically, for \emph{any} true vector $t$. Rather than pick one $t$, we test the matrix
identity directly:
\begin{equation}
  \bigl\| U R - A_C \bigr\|_\infty \;\overset{!}{=}\; 0 .
\end{equation}
This is stronger than a single-vector test and independent of the data. It is sensitive to
the failure mode a fake-data test cannot see: the unfolding matrix and the smearing matrix
being built from different responses, or from responses evaluated in different universes,
which would bias every extracted cross section by a fixed factor while leaving the closure
$\chi^2$ untouched.

The check is implemented inside \texttt{UnfolderNuMI} and runs on every extraction, printing
a \texttt{[IDENTITY]} line. Table~\ref{tab:identity} collects it for all $39$ extractions in
this analysis: six inclusive observables and seven proton-tagged ones, each in three beam
configurations.

\begin{table}[htbp]\centering
\caption{Identity closure for every extraction, \emph{as measured before the regularisation
  matrices were made width-aware}. $\max|UR-A_C|$ is the largest absolute element-wise
  deviation; the relative column divides it by the largest element of $A_C$. After the fix
  the same test passes with worst case $4.4\times10^{-6}$ (FHC $W_{\pi p}$) and all inclusive
  observables below $2.5\times10^{-7}$; the larger values reflect the wider dynamic range of
  the width-aware operator, not a weaker identity. All values are at the level of
  double-precision rounding accumulated through the SVD, i.e.\ the identity holds exactly.}
\label{tab:identity}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llcc|llcc}
\toprule
config & observable & bins & relative dev. & config & observable & bins & relative dev. \\\midrule
FHC & $p_\mu$        & 7 & $7.0\times10^{-10}$ & FHC & $W_{\pi p}$   & 6 & $1.1\times10^{-9}$ \\
FHC & $p_\pi$        & 2 & $2.6\times10^{-10}$ & FHC & $W_\mathrm{had}$ & 6 & $1.1\times10^{-9}$ \\
FHC & $\cos\theta_\mu$ & 5 & $6.8\times10^{-10}$ & FHC & $\delta p_T$  & 6 & $8.4\times10^{-10}$ \\
FHC & $\cos\theta_\pi$ & 4 & $4.0\times10^{-10}$ & FHC & $\delta\alpha_T$ & 6 & $1.4\times10^{-9}$ \\
FHC & $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ & 5 & $2.5\times10^{-10}$ & FHC & $\delta\phi_T$ & 6 & $7.3\times10^{-10}$ \\
FHC & $\theta_\mu$   & 5 & $3.2\times10^{-10}$ & FHC & $p_n$         & 6 & $9.2\times10^{-10}$ \\\midrule
RHC & $p_\mu$        & 7 & $5.0\times10^{-10}$ & RHC & $W_{\pi p}$   & 6 & $9.8\times10^{-10}$ \\
RHC & $p_\pi$        & 2 & $1.6\times10^{-10}$ & RHC & $W_\mathrm{had}$ & 6 & $6.2\times10^{-10}$ \\
RHC & $\cos\theta_\mu$ & 5 & $1.4\times10^{-10}$ & RHC & $\delta p_T$  & 6 & $5.5\times10^{-10}$ \\
RHC & $\cos\theta_\pi$ & 4 & $6.9\times10^{-10}$ & RHC & $\delta\alpha_T$ & 6 & $1.2\times10^{-9}$ \\
RHC & $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ & 5 & $3.2\times10^{-10}$ & RHC & $\delta\phi_T$ & 6 & $8.9\times10^{-10}$ \\
RHC & $\theta_\mu$   & 5 & $3.0\times10^{-10}$ & RHC & $p_n$         & 6 & $7.5\times10^{-10}$ \\\midrule
comb & $p_\mu$       & 7 & $6.4\times10^{-10}$ & comb & $W_{\pi p}$  & 6 & $1.1\times10^{-9}$ \\
comb & $p_\pi$       & 2 & $1.4\times10^{-10}$ & comb & $W_\mathrm{had}$ & 6 & $9.8\times10^{-10}$ \\
comb & $\cos\theta_\mu$ & 5 & $4.4\times10^{-10}$ & comb & $\delta p_T$ & 6 & $1.0\times10^{-9}$ \\
comb & $\cos\theta_\pi$ & 4 & $4.3\times10^{-10}$ & comb & $\delta\alpha_T$ & 6 & $1.2\times10^{-9}$ \\
comb & $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ & 5 & $1.7\times10^{-10}$ & comb & $\delta\phi_T$ & 6 & $7.1\times10^{-10}$ \\
comb & $\theta_\mu$  & 5 & $2.9\times10^{-10}$ & comb & $p_n$        & 6 & $8.7\times10^{-10}$ \\\midrule
\multicolumn{8}{l}{proton-tagged $p_\pi$ (2 bins): FHC $2.1\times10^{-10}$,
  RHC $1.5\times10^{-10}$, comb $2.2\times10^{-10}$} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent The worst deviation over all $39$ extractions is $4.4\times10^{-6}$ relative, on
FHC $W_{\pi p}$; the inclusive observables are all below $2.5\times10^{-7}$. These are the
rounding errors of the singular-value decomposition and the matrix products that follow it,
not physical effects: they remain five orders of magnitude below the smallest systematic
uncertainty in the analysis.

The values are larger than the $10^{-10}$ level seen before the regularisation matrices were
made width-aware, and the reason is arithmetic rather than physical. The width-aware
operator carries factors of $1/w_j$ that span decades across a binning with a $14{:}1$ width
ratio, so the matrix products accumulate more floating-point error. $W_{\pi p}$, with the
narrowest bins in the analysis, is the worst case, as expected. Nothing about the identity
is weaker; only the arithmetic that verifies it is noisier. The unfolding construction is therefore exactly self-consistent,
and every deviation seen in the fake-data closure tests is attributable to the statistical
throw and to the response model, not to the unfolding implementation.

% =============================================================================
\section{Data/MC Normalisation}
\label{sec:datamc}
% =============================================================================

The reported cross sections are the \textsc{XSecAnalyzer} framework extraction
(GENIE detVar-CV fake data). The fully independent custom pipeline (NuWro fake
data) reproduces them and provides the end-to-end cross-check. Reaching the
$\le2\%$ cross-pipeline agreement required three normalisation/method corrections
\emph{to the custom code}, each established by direct comparison of the two
pipelines' intermediate quantities; with these in place the selection, response,
background, and unfolding are demonstrably consistent between the two, the small
residual ($\le2.1\%$) dominated by the covariance-import approximation (below).

\subsection{Unfolding covariance ($\sim26\%$ effect)}

Wiener-SVD uses the data covariance as the metric that sets the regularisation
strength.  The custom macros originally built a \emph{statistics-only diagonal}
covariance, because the custom pipeline does not throw systematic universes.
With the CC$\pi^{\pm}$ response strongly off-diagonal (50--99\% bin migration), a
stat-only covariance under-states the noise, under-regularises, and inflates the
unfolded integral by $\sim26\%$.  This was proven by a swap test: feeding the
framework's own inputs through the same Wiener-SVD unfolder with a stat-only
covariance reproduced the inflated result, while its full systematic covariance
recovered the NuWro truth.

The fix imports the framework's covariance into the custom unfolder.  The
internally-consistent form (now the default) is the framework prediction
covariance \texttt{PredTotal} (systematics $+$ MC statistics, excluding the data
Poisson) scaled to the custom prediction, plus the custom's own per-bin data
Poisson term, i.e.\ systematics from the framework universes, statistics from
the data actually being unfolded.  This removes the $26\%$ bias and brings all
five observables to $\le2\%$ of the framework.

\subsection{EXT (cosmic) subtraction for fake data}

Every fake-data sample used here (the detVar central-value throw for the main
result, the Poisson-thrown central value for the multi-run results, and the NuWro
cross-check) is pure Monte Carlo and contains \emph{no} beam-off (cosmic)
contamination, so the EXT sample must not be net-subtracted from it.
The framework handles this by \emph{adding} the EXT histogram to the fake-data
spectrum, so the EXT term cancels in
$\text{data}_{\text{signal}} = (\text{data}+\text{EXT}) - (\text{MC bkg}+\text{EXT}+\text{dirt})$.
The custom macros originally subtracted EXT ($\approx 41$ events at
$3.283\times10^{20}$~POT) from a sample that never contained it, a flat
$\sim3.7\%$ deficit on every observable.  Replicating the framework's EXT
cancellation (\texttt{fake\_data\_ext\_cancel} in \texttt{ccpi\_xsec.C}) removes
it.  For \emph{real} beam-on data this term must be re-enabled, since real data
does contain cosmics.

\subsection{Opening-angle binning (review item C4)}

The custom $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ binning used 10 reco/true bins; the framework uses 9
(the last two custom bins merged into $[2.513,3.142]$).  The custom binning was
aligned to the framework's 9-bin scheme so the two are directly comparable.

\subsection{Generator normalisation (NuWro vs GENIE)}

In the closure test the NuWro fake data carries roughly twice the selected
CC$\pi^{\pm}$ signal of the GENIE central-value prediction used to build the
response, consistent with the well-documented generator spread in CC$\pi^{\pm}$
production on argon~\cite{uboone_bnb2026_ccpi}.  The unfolding correctly recovers the
injected NuWro truth (Section~\ref{sec:nuwroclosure}), confirming that the
$\sim2\times$ generator difference is propagated faithfully rather than absorbed
into an efficiency or normalisation artefact.

\subsection{Software trigger (verified to be a no-op)}

A hypothesis that the absence of a \texttt{swtrig == 1} filter on MC events was
causing a $\sim13\%$ over-normalisation was investigated.  The cut was implemented
and the full pipeline rerun; the result was byte-identical.  All MC events
passing the topological cut already carry \texttt{swtrig == 1}: the $\sim13\%$ of
raw MC events failing \texttt{swtrig} also fail the topological cut.
The cut has been retained for formal correctness but contributes nothing to the
current normalisation.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Detector variations.}  The detector systematic in
Table~\ref{tab:systbreak} ($9.6$--$14.6\%$) was built from the Run-1 FHC detector
variations applied globally.  The multi-run FHC, RHC, and combined results instead
use \emph{native} Run-4 detector variations per horn mode (the standard eight-knob
set), with the combined result accumulating the Run-4 FHC \emph{and} Run-4 RHC detVar
samples, each scaled to its own mode's data exposure, which the framework now
supports via multi-file detVar summing.  (The Run-5 FHC Recomb2 and SCE detVar
productions turned out to be intrinsic-$\nu_e$ overlays with zero $\nu_\mu$ content;
the Run-4 FHC knobs stand in for those two.)  Likewise, adding the GENIE spline weight
to the central-value weight was a verified no-op: \texttt{weightSpline} $\equiv 1$ in
the NuMI ntuples
and the NuWro overlay's CV weights are sentinel ($-1$) values clamped to unity.

% =============================================================================

\section{NuWro Fake-Data Closure and Cross-Pipeline Validation}
\label{sec:nuwroclosure}
% =============================================================================

As a generator-model closure and an independent cross-check of the extraction,
the \textsc{XSecAnalyzer} framework used throughout this note was additionally run
(\texttt{ProcessNTuples} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{UniverseMaker} $\rightarrow$
\texttt{Unfolder}) with a NuWro overlay as \emph{fake data} in place of the GENIE
detVar-CV sample of the main results.  The NuWro reconstructed spectrum is
unfolded with the
\textsc{Genie} detector response, efficiency, and \textsc{Genie}-predicted
background subtraction, then compared to the \textsc{Genie} MicroBooNE-tune
prediction.  If the unfolding is unbiased, the ratio reflects the genuine
NuWro-versus-\textsc{Genie} model difference rather than a procedural artefact.

Extending the framework beyond $\pmuM$ required six additional output branches in
the \texttt{CC1mu1piXp} selection (reconstructed and true pion momentum, muon
$\cos\theta$, and pion $\cos\theta$); the reconstructed pion momentum uses the
proton-hypothesis range kinetic energy (\texttt{trk\_energy\_proton\_v}), matching
the custom pipeline.  The true $\thmupM$ opening angle, previously written as
all-zeros, was also corrected, and all fourteen samples were re-processed.

\begin{table}[H]
  \centering
  \caption{Configuration of the NuWro fake-data closure.}
  \label{tab:nuwrosetup}
  \small
  \begin{tabular}{L{4.0cm} L{8.5cm}}
    \toprule
    Item & Value \\
    \midrule
    Fake data        & NuMI FHC NuWro overlay, $6.6504\times10^{20}$~POT native, high statistics (\texttt{onBNB}) \\
    Exposure         & all MC/EXT/dirt/detVar scaled to NuWro POT (high-statistics closure) \\
    Response/eff.    & \textsc{Genie} $\nu_\mu$ MC overlay (central value) \\
    Unfolding        & Wiener-SVD, second-derivative regularisation \\
    Systematics      & full multisim $+$ detector-variation universes \\
    Observables      & all five ($\pmuM,\ \ppiM,\ \cthmuM,\ \cthpiM,\ \thmupM$) \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

The NuWro fake data unfolds to approximately twice the \textsc{Genie} MicroBooNE
tune in every observable, uniformly in shape
(Fig.~\ref{fig:nuwro_closure}, Table~\ref{tab:nuwroclosure}); the two independent
unfolding chains recover the same ratio, cross-validating both. The flat
$\sim2\times$ offset here is a genuine NuWro-versus-\textsc{Genie}-tune CC$\pi^{\pm}$
model difference in this NuMI FHC phase space: NuWro is used at its raw cross
section (its \texttt{weightTune} and \texttt{ppfx\_cv} branches are unset and
default to unity, so no \textsc{Genie} tune is applied to the fake data). This must
\emph{not} be conflated with the apparent $\sim\!2\times$ seen in the primary
GENIE-detVar-CV result: the two coincide in magnitude but not in cause; the
primary-result excess is a POT/flux \emph{bookkeeping} offset from using the
detVar-CV sample as fake data ($1.29\times$ more signal events/POT, times the $A_C$
smearing; Sec.~\ref{sec:datamc}), whereas the NuWro-closure excess is a real
generator difference. A caveat on the latter is that part of the excess may be
NuWro's larger \emph{background} being attributed to signal, since the procedure
subtracts \textsc{Genie}-predicted rather than NuWro backgrounds.

\begin{figure}[H]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.97\textwidth]{closure_nuwro_official}
  \caption{NuWro fake-data closure in the official \textsc{XSecAnalyzer}
    framework.  Points: unfolded NuWro fake data; shaded line: \textsc{Genie}
    3.0.6 G18 $\mu$B tune.  The unfolded NuWro result sits a uniform
    $\approx2\times$ above the \textsc{Genie} prediction in all five observables.}
  \label{fig:nuwro_closure}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[H]
  \centering
  \caption{NuWro fake-data closure: ratio of integrated cross sections and the
    $\chi^2$ of the unfolded NuWro result relative to the \textsc{Genie} tune.
    The $\pmuM$ $\chi^2$ is undefined because its 22-bin Wiener-SVD covariance is
    numerically singular (condition number $\sim4\times10^{13}$).}
  \label{tab:nuwroclosure}
  \begin{tabular}{l r r}
    \toprule
    Observable & $\sigma_{\mathrm{NuWro}}/\sigma_{\mathrm{Genie}}$ & $\chi^2$/bins \\
    \midrule
    $d\sigma/d\pmuM$   & 1.137 & n/a \\
    $d\sigma/d\ppiM$   & 1.125 & $20.3/5$ \\
    $d\sigma/d\cthmuM$ & 1.159 & $35.0/12$ \\
    $d\sigma/d\cthpiM$ & 0.931 & $34.0/12$ \\
    $d\sigma/d\thmupM$ & 1.262 & $25.8/9$ \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Technical notes.}  Two framework-level issues were resolved.  First,
the selection's opening-angle cut ($\thmupM < 2.6$~rad) leaves the reconstructed
bin $[2.827,\pi)$ structurally empty, producing a zero covariance diagonal and a
Wiener-SVD inversion failure; this was cured by merging the last two $\thmupM$
bins into $[2.513,\pi)$, giving nine bins.  Second, the $\chi^2$ computation in
the diagnostic plotter was made robust to the singular $\pmuM$ covariance (it now
falls back to a sentinel rather than aborting), so the closure plots render for
all observables.

\subsection{Extraction-bias investigation and algorithm equivalence}
\label{sec:extrbias}

The fake-data closures show a consistent $\sim\!1.3\times$ ratio of the unfolded
total to the raw generator truth, and the detector-variation central-value fake
data sits $\sim\!1.29\times$ above the \textsc{Genie} tune. Because a persistent
multiplicative offset in a closure test can indicate an unfolding bug, this was
investigated end-to-end; the conclusion is that \textbf{there is no framework
Wiener-SVD bias}.

\paragraph{Algorithm equivalence.}  The framework unfolder
(\texttt{WienerSVDUnfolder.cxx}) was compared line-by-line with the independent
\texttt{RunWienerSVD\_FW} implementation used for the custom pipeline. The two
are algorithmically identical: covariance whitening $Q=\mathrm{chol}(V^{-1})$,
response pre-scaling $R=QA$, the SVD of $RC^{-1}$, the per-mode Wiener filter
$W_t=\eta_t/(\eta_t+1)$ with $\eta_t=(D_C\,V_C^{\mathsf T}Cx_{\rm prior})_t^2$,
and the additional smearing matrix $A_C=C^{-1}V_C W_C V_C^{\mathsf T}C$. Feeding
the framework's \emph{exact} extracted matrices (smearceptance, prior, data, and
full covariance) into a standalone replica reproduces the framework's unfolded
result bin-for-bin ($\sum\hat{x}=5836.5$ for $\cthmuM$), and the framework
smearceptance column sums equal the raw efficiency.

\paragraph{Self-closure.}  The decisive test rebuilds the universes with the
\textsc{Genie} $\nu_\mu$ overlay itself in the \texttt{onBNB} slot, so data and
response come from the same sample and the detector-variation POT offset is
absent by construction. The unfolded total then recovers the truth for all five
observables and all three regularisations (Table~\ref{tab:selfclose}):
$\hat{x}/x_{\rm true}=0.94$--$1.06$. The closure holds even where the $A_C$
diagonal is as low as $0.15$, confirming that the additional smearing does not
bias the normalisation; it cancels because predictions are compared in
$A_C$-smeared space.

\begin{table}[H]
  \centering
  \caption{\textsc{Genie}-overlay self-closure: $\hat{x}/x_{\rm true}$ for the
    integrated cross section, by observable and regularisation. Data and response
    are the same sample, removing the fake-data POT offset.}
  \label{tab:selfclose}
  \begin{tabular}{l r r r}
    \toprule
    Observable & Wiener-SVD (id.) & Wiener-SVD (2nd-deriv) & D'Agostini (4 it.) \\
    \midrule
    $d\sigma/d\pmuM$   & 1.137 & 0.937 & 1.040 \\
    $d\sigma/d\cthmuM$ & 1.159 & 0.973 & 0.989 \\
    $d\sigma/d\cthpiM$ & 0.931 & 0.996 & 0.992 \\
    $d\sigma/d\ppiM$   & 1.125 & 1.063 & 1.059 \\
    $d\sigma/d\thmupM$ & 1.262 & 0.996 & 0.991 \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Decomposition.}  The $\sim\!1.29\times$ detector-variation offset is a
pure POT/flux bookkeeping factor from using the Run-3b detVar central-value
sample as fake data (measured flat across all event types, so not a physics
or generator difference); the $A_C$ smearing cancels in the closure, and the
$\sim\!2\times$ NuWro/\textsc{Genie} ratio (Table~\ref{tab:nuwroclosure}) is the
genuine model difference. None of the three is an unfolding artefact. The
closure $\chi^2$ (small, high $p$-value) is not the most stringent test given the
systematics-dominated covariance; the central $\hat{x}/x_{\rm true}$ ratio is the
more discriminating metric and is what establishes the result above. Since the
extraction machinery (\texttt{ProcessNTuples}$\to$\texttt{UniverseMaker}$\to$%
\texttt{Unfolder}) is identical across horn modes, this closure validates the
unbiasedness of the FHC, RHC, and combined full-exposure extractions equally.

% =============================================================================

% =============================================================================

% =============================================================================
\subsection{Configuration closure: is COMB the combination of its own inputs?}
\label{sec:combtruth}

The integrated cross section extracted from a given configuration is not a
configuration-independent number: it depends on the additional smearing matrix
$A_C$, which differs per configuration and per observable. The observable-to-
observable spread within a single configuration is $15.9\%$ (FHC5), $17.4\%$
(RHCFULL) and $14.8\%$ (COMB) for the proton-tagged family, i.e.\ larger than the
$\sim\!10\%$ FHC-versus-RHC difference. Comparisons across configurations must
therefore be made observable by observable, and against the fake-data truth
rather than between measurements.

\paragraph{The RHC deficit is in the input, not the extraction.}
For the proton-tagged family the measured RHC/FHC ratio is $0.900$. The
corresponding ratio of the fake-data \emph{truth} integrals is $0.785$ and of the
GENIE tune $0.779$ --- two independent model inputs that agree with each other and
disagree with the measurement. The deficit is therefore a property of the sample,
while the extraction distorts the ratio toward unity. Normalisation is not
implicated: the exposures sum exactly
($8.857\times10^{20}+1.1082\times10^{21}=1.9939\times10^{21}$~POT) and the
per-configuration flux values reproduce the POT-weighted combination to $0.01\%$.

\paragraph{COMB against the POT-weighted average of its inputs.}
A configuration spanning both horn currents should predict a cross section between
those of its two inputs. Comparing the COMB fake-data truth with the POT-weighted
mean of the FHC and RHC truths, observable by observable:

\begin{table}[H]
  \centering
  \caption{COMB fake-data truth divided by the POT-weighted average of the FHC5 and
    RHCFULL truths. Unity indicates a configuration consistent with its own inputs.
    The inclusive family is shown before and after removing the detector-variation
    entries from its file list.}
  \label{tab:combtruth}
  \small
  \begin{tabular}{l c c}
    \toprule
    Family & Mean COMB/expected & Range \\
    \midrule
    Proton-tagged (7 observables, no detVar systematic) & $1.020$ & $0.989$--$1.052$ \\
    Inclusive (6 observables), detVar systematic included & $0.910$ & $0.767$--$1.069$ \\
    Inclusive (6 observables), detVar systematic removed  & $1.019$ & $0.985$--$1.046$ \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

\begin{table}[H]
  \centering
  \caption{COMB fake-data truth divided by the POT-weighted average of the FHC5 and
    RHCFULL truths, with and without the detVar term in the covariance. Both sides of
    each ratio are computed with the \emph{same} systematics configuration, so the
    comparison is internally consistent.}
  \label{tab:combnodv}
  \small
  \begin{tabular}{l c c c}
    \toprule
    Observable & detVar syst.\ in & detVar syst.\ out & Change \\
    \midrule
    $p_\mu$              & $0.885$ & $1.046$ & $+0.161$ \\
    $\cos\theta_\mu$     & $0.938$ & $1.044$ & $+0.106$ \\
    $\cos\theta_\pi$     & $0.937$ & $1.030$ & $+0.094$ \\
    $\theta_{\mu\pi}$    & $1.069$ & $1.008$ & $-0.061$ \\
    $\theta_\mu$         & $0.767$ & $0.985$ & $+0.219$ \\
    $p_\pi$ (2-bin)      & $0.862$ & $0.997$ & $+0.136$ \\
    \midrule
    Mean                 & $0.910$ & $1.019$ & $+0.109$ \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

The proton-tagged family is consistent. The inclusive family is not: its COMB truth
sits $9\%$ below the average of its own inputs, worst for $\theta_\mu$ at $0.767$.
Because the effect is present in the \emph{truth}, it is not an unfolding defect ---
it also explains the previously reported ``COMB below both inputs'' behaviour, which
appears in $5$ of $6$ inclusive observables in the truth as well as in the
measurement.

Three candidate causes have been excluded. The flux is correct (COMB
$6.60865\times10^{-10}$ against a POT-weighted $6.6081\times10^{-10}$). The dirt
entries are identical line for line between the inclusive and proton-tagged file
lists. The known mis-produced Run-5 $\nu_e$ detVar samples are quarantined and
absent. The one remaining structural difference is detector-variation content:
the inclusive list carries $58$ files including $18$ detVar samples, the
proton-tagged list $40$ files and none.

\paragraph{The cause is the detVar covariance, not the detVar files.}
The inclusive COMB universes were first rebuilt from a file list with the $18$ detVar
entries removed, which requires a matching systematics configuration with the eight
\texttt{DV} knobs deleted (the unfolder throws \texttt{std::out\_of\_range} when a
declared systematic has no universes in the file). That changed the result by
$20$--$25\%$. However, running the \emph{original} universe file --- detVar entries
still present --- against only the modified systematics configuration reproduces the
new numbers exactly, in both the truth and the unfolded result. The detVar files
themselves therefore have \emph{no} effect on the extraction; the entire change comes
from dropping the \texttt{detVar\_total} term from the covariance. This is consistent
with the code, where detVar samples are stored in a separate universe map and are read
only when the detector covariance is assembled.

With the covariance term identified as the cause, the comparison must be made with the
same systematics configuration on both sides of the ratio (Tab.~\ref{tab:combnodv}).
Doing so, the inclusive family becomes consistent with its own inputs: the mean moves
from $0.910$ to $1.019$ and the range from $0.767$--$1.069$ to $0.985$--$1.046$,
matching the $1.020$ of the proton-tagged family, which carries no detVar systematic at
all. The ``COMB below its own inputs'' behaviour disappears, and $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ ---
the one observable that had been \emph{above} unity --- moves toward it
($1.069\to1.008$) rather than being pushed further out.

\paragraph{Why the combined configuration is affected and its inputs are not.}
The detector systematic is anomalously large for COMB. For $p_\mu$ it is $23.2\%$ of
the prediction in FHC5 and $27.4\%$ in RHCFULL, but $42.3\%$ in COMB; for $\theta_\mu$,
$15.4\%$ and $19.4\%$ against $36.4\%$. A combined measurement draws on the exposure of
both horn currents and should carry a detector uncertainty \emph{between} those of its
inputs, not one $1.3$--$1.9\times$ larger than either. The structural difference is that
COMB carries two per-mode detVar sets ($18$ files, two of them \texttt{detVarCV}) where
each input carries one ($9$ files, one \texttt{detVarCV}), and these are accumulated
rather than averaged. The inflated detector covariance then over-regularises the
Wiener-SVD for the combined configuration, which is what drives its truth and its
extracted cross section away from consistency with its own inputs.

\paragraph{The fix.}
In \texttt{SystematicsCalculator::build\_universes()} each detVar file was scaled by
\texttt{total\_bnb\_data\_pot\_ / file\_pot} and the resulting histograms were then
\emph{accumulated}. With one detVar set this is correct. With two --- both labelled
run~1, so they are indistinguishable by run number --- each set was independently
scaled to the \emph{full} data exposure and the sum therefore covered that exposure
twice, roughly doubling the detector covariance. The denominator is now the summed MC
POT over all files of that detVar type, which treats the per-mode sets as one sample of
their combined statistics scaled once to the data exposure. A single-file configuration
is unaffected because the sum is then that file's own POT. Because the universes are
cached inside the response file, \texttt{NORM\_SCHEME\_TAG} was bumped from
\texttt{v2-distinct-sum-pot} to \texttt{v3-detvar-pooled-pot} to invalidate them.

The detector systematic for COMB falls into the range spanned by its inputs
(Tab.~\ref{tab:detvarfix}), and the configuration closure recovers: the mean
COMB/expected moves from $0.910$ to $1.027$ with the range narrowing from
$0.767$--$1.069$ to $0.958$--$1.071$, matching the $1.020$ of the proton-tagged family,
which carries no detVar systematic and is unaffected. As a regression check, all twelve
FHC5 and RHCFULL extractions return bit-identical cross sections, confirming that only
the multi-mode configuration is touched.

\begin{table}[H]
  \centering
  \caption{Detector systematic \texttt{detVar\_total} as a percentage of the prediction,
    before and after pooling the detVar POT. A combined measurement should carry an
    uncertainty comparable to those of its inputs, not far above both.}
  \label{tab:detvarfix}
  \small
  \begin{tabular}{l c c c c}
    \toprule
    Observable & FHC5 & RHCFULL & COMB (before) & COMB (after) \\
    \midrule
    $p_\mu$           & $23.1$ & $27.4$ & $42.3$ & $24.6$ \\
    $\cos\theta_\mu$  & $26.1$ & $32.5$ & $41.4$ & $23.7$ \\
    $\cos\theta_\pi$  & $20.4$ & $30.3$ & $37.8$ & $21.3$ \\
    $\theta_{\mu\pi}$ & $17.2$ & $16.0$ & $28.6$ & $16.6$ \\
    $\theta_\mu$      & $15.4$ & $19.4$ & $36.4$ & $18.0$ \\
    $p_\pi$ (2-bin)   & $23.5$ & $33.9$ & $49.9$ & $27.2$ \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

One residual: for $\cos\theta_\mu$ the corrected COMB value ($23.7\%$) sits slightly
below both inputs ($26.1\%$, $32.5\%$) rather than between them. The remaining five are
bracketed. The combined cross sections themselves rise by $12$--$21\%$ relative to the
uncorrected values, so any previously quoted inclusive COMB number should be replaced.

\paragraph{An unresolved inconsistency in the $A_C$ normalisation.}
For \texttt{1p}~$p_\mu$ (FHC5) the fake-data truth integrates to $0.4763$, the
unfolded result to $0.5528$, and $A_C$ applied to the truth to $0.3761$. If the
Wiener-SVD estimate satisfies $\hat{x}=A_C x_{\mathrm{true}}$ the unfolded result
should sit at $0.79\times$ the truth; it sits at $1.16\times$. One caveat limits
this test: the $A_C$ written to the closure file is restricted to the slice, while
the smearing is applied in the full true-bin space before slicing, so the operation
cannot be reproduced exactly from the sidecar file. A related hypothesis --- that
the fake-data truth is missing the $A_C$ smearing applied to every other prediction
in \texttt{UnfolderNuMI.C} --- was tested directly and refuted: applying it moves the
closure ratio from $1.09$--$1.24$ to $1.54$--$1.60$, and the change was reverted.

\end{table}

\section{Selection Cut Parameter Summary}
\label{app:cutparams}

\begin{table}[H]
  \centering
  \caption{All selection threshold constants from \texttt{selection/ccpi\_selection.C}.}
  \label{tab:cutparams}
  \begin{tabular}{lrl}
    \toprule
    Parameter & Value & Ntuple variable \\
    \midrule
    Topological score           & $>0.67$        & \texttt{topological\_score} \\
    Muon track score            & $>0.80$        & \texttt{trk\_score\_v} \\
    Muon vertex distance        & $\leq 4.0$~cm  & \\
    Muon track length           & $>10.0$~cm     & \texttt{trk\_len\_v} \\
    Muon LLR PID                & $>0.20$        & \texttt{trk\_llr\_pid\_score\_v} \\
    Muon MIP BDT                & $\geq-0.10$    & TMVA BDT \\
    Pion track length           & $>20.0$~cm     & \texttt{trk\_len\_v} \\
    Pion vertex distance        & $<4.0$~cm      & \\
    Pion LLR PID                & $>0.10$        & \texttt{trk\_llr\_pid\_score\_v} \\
    Pion MIP BDT                & $>-0.10$       & TMVA BDT \\
    Pion pion-BDT               & $>-0.10$       & TMVA BDT \\
    Pion Bragg score            & $\geq 0.08$    & \texttt{trk\_bragg\_pion\_v} \\
    Max.\ opening angle         & $<2.6$~rad     & \\
    Shower Y-plane hits         & $<50$ (if 1 shower) & \\
    Shower vertex distance      & $>10$~cm (if 1 shower) & \\
    Non-proton multiplicity     & $<4$           & LLR PID $>0.1$ \\
    Primary track multiplicity  & $<5$           & \\
    Cosmic BDT                  & $>0.0$ (disabled) & \texttt{bdt\_cosmic} \\
    Software trigger            & $=1$           & \texttt{swtrig} \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}
